> **FINAL ARCHITECTURAL MANDATE**:  
> Dialogue models are dynamic kinetic instruments. The Director and Screenwriter must choose the appropriate Energy Vector (Harmonic, Frictional, Tactical, Metaphysical) and Interaction Geometry (Models 1–8, 0) that serves the authentic emotional core of the specific story being told, whether an epic drama, a tender romance, a snappy comedy, or an adrenaline-fueled heist.


## 🏛️ PART IX: LINE C (GREEK CHORUS), PROPS, ESCALATION & RESOLUTION BANKS

## § LINE C — GREEK CHORUS (Crowd / Audience / Mass)

> **Definition:** Line C characters are not meaningless extras — they are **the voice of society** looking into the story with incomplete information. Their misjudgment creates **Dramatic Irony** — the most powerful emotion in drama.

### Line C's Narrative Role

| Function | Mechanism | Emotional Effect |
|---|---|---|
| **Social Mirror** | Reflects collective bias (money = competent, silence = weakness) | Audience sees injustice — hurts for the protagonist |
| **Unwitting Witness** | Present but lacking full context | Protagonist is judged publicly without being allowed to defend |
| **Contrast Layer** | Line A silently endures / Line C loudly misjudges | Contrast highlights Character A's inner world |
| **Living Texture** | Creates the feeling of a world operating around the story | Setting is not a set — it is the real world |

### Line C's Judgment Principles

1. **They always judge by surface** — no context of Character A/B's inner world
2. **They usually judge wrongly** — criticize the protagonist, praise the antagonist
3. **They don't know they're wrong** — speak naturally, confidently, without malice
4. **Their judgment reflects real social bias** — not villains, just ordinary people

### Designing Line C in the Script

**No individual names.** Describe by group characteristics:
- Estimated number: `1 male vendor ~40`, `3 frightened villagers`, `a loose group of 8`
- Collective emotion/attitude: fearful, gossiping, admiring, criticizing
- Collective action: no close-up faces, no individual naming

**No reference image needed.** Context description is sufficient for Omni Flash to render correctly.

### Appearance Rhythm by Beat

### Appearance Rhythm by Beat (6-Beat Master Arc)

| Beat | How Line C appears |
|---|---|
| **B1 — HOOK (3%)** | Absent / or 1 distant blurred silhouette — atmosphere texture only |
| **B2 — SETUP (12%)** | Present unintentionally — unknowing witnesses to the old world and flawed status quo |
| **B3 — DRAMA (35%)** | Sparse background / whispers — giving central stage to Tier A vs. Tier B1 ideological clash (60% dialogue) |
| **B4 — CLIMAX (30%)** | Stunned breathless observers or **COMPLETELY ABSENT** — solitary tragic vacuum for Tier A's Ego Death |
| **B5 — RELEASE (12%)** | 1-2 people passing by or safe haven space — Tier B2 companion provides unconditional grace |
| **B6 — ECHO (8%)** | **VIBRANT LIVING COMMUNITY** — Tier A harmoniously integrates into Tier C society under open sky |

### Line C (Tier C) Flexible Staging & Dialogue Modes

> **Flexible Line C Principle (Tuyến C Linh Động Theo Bối Cảnh & Cốt Truyện)**: While Line A (A1/A2) and Line B (Tier B satellites) are capped at **1 to maximum 4 named characters per scene**, Line C (Tier C background extras: coworkers, café patrons, waiters, passersby, crowd, guards) scales **flexibly** according to the scene's setting (*0 in private home rooms; natural ambient/reactive presence at work, on an outing, on a date, or at public events*).
> When Line C speaks or reacts, the Director chooses between two optical modes depending on the dramatic beat:
> 1. **Off-Screen / Foreground-Silhouette Chorus (O.S.)**: Camera holds on the protagonist (A1) while Line C's whispers or comments ring out from off-screen or past blurred foreground shoulders (isolating A1's inner reaction).
> 2. **Group Reaction Cut (Medium Group Shot / Wide Shot)**: Camera cuts briefly to the witnessing group/crowd reacting collectively to a public revelation or confrontation (without assigning individual 16:9 character model sheets to Line C extras).

**Line C Dialogue Conditions:**
- ≤ 80 characters/line — short, passing like casual gossip
- Tone: whispered, calm, gossiping — NOT dramatic, NOT loud
- Maximum 2 Lines C dialogue in 1 clip (15 seconds)

**Example dialogue — correct technique:**

| Context | Line C says | Truth the audience knows |
|---|---|---|
| Father returns after 15 years | `"Empty hands. Typical."` | He gave up everything to not become a burden |
| Protagonist silently absorbs hurt | `"She should just kick him out."` | She's trying to keep the only person she has left |
| Antagonist smiling, generous | `"He's so generous. Nothing like his brother."` | That is a mask hiding calculation |
| Crowd flees in fear | `"Run — just run!"` | Protagonist cannot run — someone still needs to be saved |

---

## § MASTER CINEMATIC PROP & TWIST INTEGRATION ARCHITECTURE

> 📖 **Full Repertoire References**:
> - `d:\Project\Sven\engine\references\1-narrative-screenplay-architecture.md` (20 Twist Archetypes, 5 Pillars, 10 Case Studies)
> - `d:\Project\Sven\engine\references\1-narrative-screenplay-architecture.md` (Master Twist Screenwriting Skill)

### 🎭 The 7 Genre-Driven Prop Archetypes:
Writers select the prop archetype that fits the genre and dramatic stakes of the project:
1. **Archetype 1: Emotional / Karmic Anchor (Wound ➔ Echo Prop)**:
   - *Core Function*: Represents psychological trauma, defensive ego, fear, or entrapment (e.g., rusted padlock, chipped dagger, debt ledger, unmailed letter) ➔ Undergoes a 180° physical reversal into spiritual enlightenment and grace (Beat 6). *Primary for Drama, Karma, Family, and Redemption.*
2. **Archetype 2: Tactical / Weapon / Survival Prop**:
   - *Core Function*: Measures survival urgency and conflict escalation through observable physical wear-and-tear (e.g., firearm jamming or down to final bullet, Zippo running out of fluid, dull blade chipped on armor). *Primary for Action, War, Thriller, Survival.*
3. **Archetype 3: Transactional / Evidence / Leverage Prop**:
   - *Core Function*: Determines the balance of power, blackmail, or legal retribution through custody and decryption (e.g., encrypted flash drive, forged contract, stolen family signet ring, audio cassette). *Primary for Crime, Noir, Legal, Corporate.*
4. **Archetype 4: MacGuffin / High-Stakes Catalyst**:
   - *Core Function*: The coveted asset driving the entire narrative pursuit that all factions collide to seize (e.g., briefcase with detonation codes, sole viral antidote, ancient navigation relic). *Primary for Heist, Adventure, Sci-Fi, Spy.*
5. **Archetype 5: Relational / Romantic Keepsake**:
   - *Core Function*: Physical metric of intimacy, distance, sacrifice, or broken vows between two souls (e.g., shared umbrella left behind in rain, heirloom pendant split in half, wedding band). *Primary for Romance, Melodrama, Bromance.*
6. **Archetype 6: Situational / Environmental Prop**:
   - *Core Function*: Everyday ambient object seized reflexively during mortal jeopardy to invert power dynamics in a single take (e.g., shattered bottle neck, heavy crystal ashtray, wrench). *Universal across all high-tension confrontations.*
7. **Archetype 7: Comic / Subversive / Contrast Prop**:
   - *Core Function*: Incongruous, mundane, or absurd item placed into life-and-death stakes to puncture tension and subvert expectations (e.g., rubber squeak toy dropped during infiltration). *Primary for Dark Comedy, Action Comedy, Satire.*

### 🔄 The 4 Series Prop Lifecycles (Phân bổ Vòng đời trong Phim dài tập):
1. **Macro Series Arc**: Spans from Episode 1 through Final Episode (e.g., classic Wound ➔ Echo Prop, ancestral sword, cursed ring).
2. **Micro Episode Arc**: Self-contained within a single 10s clip or single episode (e.g., episodic murder weapon, bomb defusal kit, temporary hostage leverage).
3. **Relay / Hand-off Chain**: Continuously switches hands between Protagonist, Antagonist, and Ally, flipping the tactical upper hand with every exchange.
4. **Ticking Clock / Depletion**: Progressively degrades or empties across time (oxygen meter dropping 100% ➔ 5%, battery flashing red, ammo depleted).

### ⚡ Prop-to-Twist Mechanical Coupling:
Every major twist (TW-01 to TW-20) MUST be physically anchored into an observable prop state:
- **Decoy Swap (`TW-01`, `TW-02`)**: Container opens to reveal substituted counterfeit goods.
- **Micro-Detail Reveal (`TW-05`, `TW-16`)**: Camera pushes in on engraved initials or blood trace, demolishing an alibi.
- **Weapon Backfire (`TW-07`)**: Antagonist's bespoke weapon jams or reverses against them.
- **Prop Profile**: Material → Shape/Size → Color/Texture → Weight/How held → Opening State → Escalation State → Payoff Transformed State.
- **"the only [prop name] in frame"**: Mandatory anti-duplication suffix.

---

## § SHOW, DON'T TELL & THERMAL POLARITY

- Emotions, motivations, lessons ALL must be **encoded into specific actions / objects / lighting / biomechanics**.
- NO subtitles, captions, or explanatory voiceover describing abstract emotions.
- **The Thermal Polarity Law**:
  - Cold Phase (Beat 4.3): Monochrome cold **chilly pale daylight** (slate grey, snow, rain, shadow) + ragged breathing.
  - Warm Phase (Beat 5.1): Soft golden warmth **warm amber glow** (amber firelight, morning sun, steam) + `[pause: 1.5s]` silence + warm whisper.
- **Echo Mirror Technique**: Beat 6 reverses opening compositions from Beat 1/Beat 2 — delivering visual closure and organic recognition of change.

---

## § ESCALATION BANK — CONFLICT TYPES

> Agent MUST choose 1 main conflict type for the DRAMA phase (Beat 3). Add to metadata: `CONFLICT TYPE: [#N] — [name]`. Anti-repetition: don't repeat the same type twice consecutively in a session.

### Group A — Interpersonal Conflict

| # | Conflict Type | Escalation Mechanism | Suitable Genre |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Betrayal & Breach of Trust** | A trusted person uses information/position to sabotage → escalates as victim discovers layer by layer | Drama, Thriller |
| A2 | **Power Struggle** | Both sides have legitimate reasons to claim control → escalates through small win-loss cycles | Politics, Business, Family |
| A3 | **Sacrifice vs. Self-Preservation** | One side wants to help, the other fears exploitation → escalates through stacked misunderstandings | Romance, Humanist |
| A4 | **Blackmail / Covert Control** | Person with secret information gradually tightens control → victim is forced to concede step by step | Thriller, Crime |
| A5 | **Wrongful Accusation** | Character is falsely accused → every self-defense action is reinterpreted as evidence of guilt | Psychological, Courtroom |

### Group B — Internal Conflict

| # | Conflict Type | Escalation Mechanism | Suitable Genre |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | **Logic vs. Emotion** | Character knows the right thing but emotion pulls the other way → escalates through each wrong choice | Drama, Psychological |
| B2 | **Identity Threatened** | Character forced to act against their own nature → escalates through each small concession | Psychological, Social |
| B3 | **Accumulated Guilt** | Past wrong action gradually resurfaces → escalates through haunting/flashback/delayed consequences | Psychological, Tragedy |
| B4 | **Moral Dilemma with No Exit** | Every choice hurts someone → escalates through progressively worse choices | Ethics, Heavy Drama |

### Group C — Situational Conflict

| # | Conflict Type | Escalation Mechanism | Suitable Genre |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Race Against Time** | Hard deadline with clear consequences → escalates through each obstacle slowing progress | Action, Thriller |
| C2 | **Resource Depletion** | Money/time/stamina/trust running out → escalates through each loss forcing strategic change | Survival, Drama |
| C3 | **Secret Leaking Gradually** | Hidden information leaks piece by piece → escalates as each revealed piece pushes character into harder situation | Thriller, Family |
| C4 | **Hostile Environment** | Physical/social setting constantly creates new barriers → escalates through layers of danger/pressure | Action, Light Horror |

---

## § RESOLUTION BANK — ENDING TYPES

> Resolution (Beat 5–6) is the **natural consequence** from the character arc + chosen conflict — NOT a random event from outside. Add to metadata: `RESOLUTION TYPE: [#N] — [name]`.

| # | Resolution Type | Application Condition | Target Emotion |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | **Triumph (Clear Victory)** | Character overcomes conflict through growth/right action | Excited, satisfied |
| R2 | **Pyrrhic Victory (Win but Lose)** | Conflict resolved but lost something important to do it | Bittersweet, lingering |
| R3 | **Tragic Downfall** | Complete Negative Arc — character doesn't change and faces full consequences | Haunting, heavy |
| R4 | **Redemption** | Character who did wrong now does right — even if late — to repair | Moved, hopeful |
| R5 | **Open Ending** | Conflict not fully resolved, but character has changed enough to continue | Curious, wanting more |
| R6 | **Ironic Reversal** | Character gets what they wanted but in a completely unexpected way — consequence of own choices | Shocked + reflective |
| R7 | **Sacrifice (Meaningful)** | Character gives up what matters most to protect others / a greater value | Deeply moved, respectful |
| R8 | **Status Quo Restored + Changed** | World returns to normal but character has changed irreversibly | Matured, quietly profound |

**Resolution Rules:**
- Visualized through the **180° Transformed Echo Prop** planted in Beat 2 (Setup).
- Natural causality from the character's own choices — not divine deus ex machina.
- Beat 6 (Echo) concludes with a **closing image that answers the opening image** (a spoken maxim only with a `BREAK:` line — Doctrine §8.8).

---

## § STEP 0 — IDEA GENERATION (When user request is vague)

> When user has no specific idea, agent outputs **5 distinct Ideas** → waits for user to choose 1 → only then begins Stage 1.

### Format for 5 Ideas

```
IDEA #[N]
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
GENRE: [Drama / Action / Psychological / ...]
SUMMARY: [1 sentence — who, does what, what consequence]
PROTAGONIST: [1-sentence description — defining trait + core weakness]
CONFLICT: [Type + 1-sentence description]
TWIST HINT: [1-sentence hint — don't fully reveal]
TARGET EMOTION: [Emotion audience will feel after watching]
```

### Criteria — 5 Ideas Must Satisfy
1. **Different genres**: no 2 ideas with the same genre
2. **Different conflict types**: each idea uses a different conflict group (A/B/C)
3. **Different character arcs**: each idea has a different arc type
4. **Different target emotional tones**: no 2 ideas with the same emotion
5. **Has twist potential**: each idea must suggest at least 1 unexpected turning point

---

## § NARRATIVE STRUCTURE — 7 TYPES

> Default is Structure A. AI is allowed and encouraged to randomly select B-G, prioritizing non-repetition of recently used structures.

| # | Structure | Arrangement | When it works |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Standard Linear | Hook (3%) → Setup (12%) → Drama (35%) → Climax (30%) → Release (12%) → Echo (8%) | Safe, clear, universal |
| **B** | Mirror Loop | Opens with image of character in initial state → 6 Beats execute → Closes with exact opening frame inverted | Character transforms deeply |
| **C** | Consequence First | Opens with Climax/Resolution moment (reason unclear) → flashes back to Setup/Drama → Climax decodes → Echo closes | Strong curiosity, mystery |
| **D** | Dual Parallel | Intercuts between Character A and Character B moving toward convergence at Beat 4 Climax | High tension, dual protagonist |
| **E** | Witness Narrator | Supporting Tier C character at Hook as observer → story unfolds → returns to witness in Echo | Framing device layer |
| **F** | Cause Across Time | Compress generational cause in Setup → Climax across decades → Echo shows modern harmony | Multi-generational depth |
| **G** | Single Moment Compression | Hook and Setup merge into single condensed space-time unit → rapid escalation → Climax & Echo | High-intensity short format |

---

## § TWIST BANK — 12 CINEMATIC TYPES & 3-WAVE CLIMAX ANATOMY

> **Same catalog as Part IV, shorter names.** Record the TW code in the Spine TWIST row (Doctrine §9.9): Consequence → TW-02/14 · Identity → TW-05 · Perspective → TW-04/08 · Reverse Role → TW-09/12 · Hidden Kindness → TW-10/13 · Symbolic → TW-19 · Full Circle → TW-11 · Moral Awakening → TW-15 · Redemption → TW-06 · Forgiveness → TW-07 · Redirect → TW-03/20 · Secret Connection → TW-16. The 3-wave climax is one option; the twist budget is one major plus one or two minor per ~100 s episode (§9.7).

#### 🎬 4-STAGE DYNAMIC TWIST PROGRESSION
1. **Stage 1: Setup (Planting Belief & Wound Prop)** [Beats 1–2]: Plant the cognitive gap, flawed premise, and physical Wound Prop.
2. **Stage 2: Misdirect (False Confidence & Shadow Maneuvers)** [Beat 3]: Reinforce protagonist's false control; Antagonist seals exits.
3. **Stage 3: 3-Wave Climax Explosion (Ego Death & Anagnorisis)** [Beat 4]:
   - *Wave 1 (Identity / Trap)*: The supposed victim or ally executes the real trap.
   - *Wave 2 (Physical / Loss)*: Material, bodily, or structural devastation.
   - *Wave 3 (Karma & Ego Death)*: Protagonist recognizes they created their own doom. Total collapse.
4. **Stage 4: Payoff & Transformation (Reverse Rescue & Echo Prop)** [Beats 5–6]: Unexpected grace + 180° prop transformation + closing image.

| # | Twist Type | Description & Reversal Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Consequence Twist** | Character's action causes the opposite of what they wanted — their own trap springs on them |
| 2 | **Identity Twist** | True identity revealed — person thought to be X turns out to be Y |
| 3 | **Perspective Twist** | Shift viewpoint — replay/reveal true motive behind a misunderstood action |
| 4 | **Reverse Role Twist** | Person thought to be strong/saving — is actually being saved/dependent on the other |
| 5 | **Hidden Kindness Twist** | Person who was hated/driven away was actually silently sacrificing to protect |
| 6 | **Symbolic Twist** | Symbolic prop insert-shot (*Chekhov's Gun*) reveals hidden meaning planted from the start |
| 7 | **Full Circle Twist** | Ending repeats the opening image but carries completely new meaning |
| 8 | **Moral Awakening** | Character collapses/awakens to realize their mistake when it's too late |
| 9 | **Redemption Twist** | Character who did something bad now performs a surprising good action at the last moment |
| 10 | **Forgiveness Twist** | Forgiveness instead of revenge — moral direction bends against expectations |
| 11 | **Redirect Twist** | Change the direction of outcome — what was expected to go to A instead goes to B |
| 12 | **Secret Connection** | Two people/events are connected to each other from before — neither (nor the audience) knew |

---

## § SCENE & DURATION ALLOCATION — ELASTIC DUAL-ATOM (`10.0s` & `15.0s`) & ACT-SCALED EPISODE ENGINE
# (HỆ THỐNG PHÂN BỔ SỐ CẢNH & THỜI LƯỢNG ĐỘNG THEO HỒI VÀ ĐỘNG CƠ THỂ LOẠI)

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **ELASTIC DUAL-ATOM LAW & ZERO CUMULATIVE TIMESTAMPS (`Gate 3M`)**:
> 1. Every scene clip starts at **`00:00.0`** and runs to either **`00:10.0` (`[🎭 STANDARD · 10.0s]` / `[⚡ SEEDANCE · 10.0s]`)** or **`00:15.0` (`[⚡ SEEDANCE · 15.0s]` / `[🎭 EXTENDED RECKONING · 15.0s]`)** (or `00:30.0` on explicit request). Never use cumulative episode timestamps (`[00:10–00:20]`) in prompts.
> 2. **Ban on Uniform Episode Lengths**: Total duration $T_{\text{ep}}$ of an episode is the sum of its `10.0s` and `15.0s` clips:
>    $$\mathbf{T_{\text{ep}} = (N_{10} \times 10.0\text{s}) + (N_{15} \times 15.0\text{s})}$$
>    where both total scene count ($M = N_{10} + N_{15} \in [6, 14]$) and the `10.0s : 15.0s` ratio scale dynamically by **Episode Archetype (`Archetypes 1–5`)** and **Genre Engine (`Engines 1–6`, Part II §5)**.

### Step 1 — Select Format, Genre Structural Engine (`1–6`) & Episode Archetype (`1–5`)

1. **Format A — Standalone Short Film (`1 Episode` · `6–12 Scenes` · `65s–135s`)**:
   - **Compact Short (`6–7 Scenes` · `65s–75s`)**: `Sc 01` Hook (`10s`) ➔ `Sc 02` Setup (`10s`) ➔ `Sc 03–04` 3-Wall Escalation (`10s/15s`) ➔ `Sc 05` 3-Wave Climax (`15s`) ➔ `Sc 06` Release & 180° Echo (`10s/15s`).
   - **Standard Short (`8–10 Scenes` · `85s–115s`)**: `Sc 01` Hook (`10s`) ➔ `Sc 02` Setup (`10s`) ➔ `Sc 03–05` 3-Wall Escalation (`10s/15s`) ➔ `Sc 06–07` Climax (`15s`) ➔ `Sc 08` Release (`10s`) ➔ `Sc 09` 180° Echo (`10s`).
2. **Format B — Multi-Episode Series (`N Episodes` · Scaled by Act Archetype)**:
   - **Archetype 1 (`Act I: Ignition & Trap`)**: **`6–8 Scenes` (`65s–85s` · `85% 10s : 15% 15s`)** — Fast hook, wound/prop plant, inciting assault, Point-of-No-Return cliffhanger (or First Mini-Slap in `Engine 2 Microdrama`).
   - **Archetype 2 (`Act IIA: Escalation & False Dawn`)**: **`8–9 Scenes` (`85s–100s` · `80% 10s : 20% 15s`)** — Counter-plan, `B/C-Plot` cross-cut, False Dawn / Mid-Boss Slap / Gauntlet Clash, Cold Water Slap.
   - **Archetype 3 (`Act IIB: Midpoint Shatter & Betrayal`)**: **`9–11 Scenes` (`100s–125s` · `70% 10s : 30% 15s`)** — Suspense lead-in, Mid-Episode Twist/Betrayal (`15s`), High-Motion `Seedance` Chase/Clash (`15s`), Pronoun/Status Rupture.
   - **Archetype 4 (`Act IIIA: Siege & Dark Night`)**: **`8–10 Scenes` (`95s–115s` · `70% 10s : 30% 15s`)** — Sealed exits, `State 2 Prop Fracture`, `15s Track A-Locked` Dark Night, Option C Awakening.
   - **Archetype 5 (`Act IIIB + IV Unified Finale`)**: **`10–14 Scenes` (`115s–155s` · `65% 10s : 35% 15s`)** — `70–75%` 3-Wave Grand Showdown (`3–5 × 15s` clips) + `25–30%` Thermal Shift, Earned Cost, `State 3 Echo Prop`, and Bookend Closing Image (**Zero Cliffhanger**).

---

### Step 2 — Mandatory Episode Header Metadata Output

```text
FORMAT                : [Standalone Short Film / Multi-Episode Series (Ep N of M)]
GENRE STRUCTURAL ENG. : [Engine 1 (Drama) / Engine 2 (Microdrama) / Engine 3 (Action) / Engine 4 (Thriller) / Engine 5 (Sitcom) / Engine 6 (Healing)]
EPISODE ARCHETYPE     : [Archetype 1 (Act I) / Archetype 2 (Act IIA) / Archetype 3 (Act IIB) / Archetype 4 (Act IIIA) / Archetype 5 (Act IIIB+IV Finale)]
SCENE & DURATION MIX  : [M Scenes Total = N10 × 10.0s Standard + N15 × 15.0s Extended/Seedance = Total Seconds]
HOOK & BRIDGE         : Hook #[1–12] ([Name]) ➔ [Object / Person / Spatial / Reverse Angle / Atmospheric Bridge]
TIER-1 PROP STATE     : [State 0 Intact (Act I) / State 1 Degraded (Act II) / State 2 Fractured Renamed (Act IIIA) / State 3 180° Echo Rebirth (Finale)]
VOICE MODE SYMPHONY   : [Sc 01 Model # ➔ Sc 02 Model # ➔ ... ➔ Sc M Model # (≥ 4–5 distinct modes; zero >2 consecutive repeats)]
PRONOUN REGISTER ARC  : [System 1–4: Baseline ➔ Friction ➔ Rupture / Resolution]
```

## 🏛️ PART X: THE ASYMMETRICAL 13-BEAT MAP, VIRAL ADDICTION ENGINES & HIGH-STAKES REVERSALS

> **SSOT INTEGRATION**: Self-contained master architecture for viral microdramas, high-retention short series, and dynamic episodic screenwriting.

### § 1. The Retention Curve & The 13 Asymmetrical Weighted Beats
Uniform pacing (twelve equal 8.3% blocks) destroys retention because viewer drop-off is steepest at seconds 15–90. Pacing must be asymmetrical:

```
[0% ──► 3%]   BEAT 1: COLD HOOK ──────────────────► [ULTRA-DENSE] Immediate shock; 0 explanation; instant cognitive gap.
[3% ──► 10%]  BEAT 2: BASELINE PRESENCE ──────────► [DENSE] Sensory immersion, mundane living reality; 0 biography dump.
[10% ──► 18%] BEAT 3: INCITING CATALYST ─────────► [DENSE] Peace shattered; unavoidable moral fracture.
[18% ──► 30%] BEAT 4: FIRST COMMITMENT ──────────► [MEDIUM] World expansion, relationship friction, initial obstacle.
[30% ──► 38%] BEAT 5: ESCALATION & RESISTANCE ───► [MEDIUM] Stakes increase; opponent counters with systemic force.
[38% ──► 45%] BEAT 6: THE CENTRAL REVERSAL ──────► [ULTRA-DENSE] Midpoint twist detonates; sharp, concise, irreversible.
[45% ──► 52%] BEAT 7: SHOCKWAVE EXPANSION ───────► [MEDIUM] Consequences ripple outward; collateral damage lands.
[52% ──► 60%] BEAT 8: THE TRAP CLOSES ───────────► [DENSE] Adversary corners protagonist; exits sealed.
[60% ──► 68%] BEAT 9: PEAK DESPAIR ──────────────► [DENSE] Maximum loss; sacred relic broken or stolen.
[68% ──► 78%] BEAT 10: DARK NIGHT OF THE SOUL ───► [DEEPEST LULL] Deliberate deceleration; zero action; profound insight.
[78% ──► 83%] BEAT 11: REKINDLED INTERNAL WILL ──► [DENSE, SHORT] Audacious moral decision; tempo accelerates sharply.
[83% ──► 94%] BEAT 12: THE GRAND CLIMAX ─────────► [DENSEST IN ENTIRE NARRATIVE] Truth bomb, legal exposure, moral triumph.
[94% ──► 100%] BEAT 13: CIRCULAR EPILOGUE ───────► [EXPANSIVE SETTLE] Resolution; circular callback to Beat 1 with inverted meaning.
```

### § 2. The 6 Psychological Addiction Engines
1. **Open Loop Protocol**: Every scene, beat, and episode (except the final epilogue) MUST conclude with an unanswered question, unexamined object, or suspended consequence. Never end mid-story on a resolved plateau.
2. **The 3-Phase Thread-Callback Architecture (Plant ➔ Deepen ➔ Reverse)**:
   - *Phase 1 (Plant)*: Motif prop/detail appears innocuously without spotlighting.
   - *Phase 2 (Deepen)*: Reappears with high emotional stake attached.
   - *Phase 3 (Payoff & Inversion)*: Re-emerges in Climax where its true legal/moral reality detonates the plot.
3. **Emotional Sine-Wave Oscillation**: Systematically alternate hope vs. crushing obstacles (*Hope ➔ Obstacle ➔ Small Win ➔ Crushing Blow ➔ Despair ➔ Insight ➔ Triumph*) to eliminate emotional numbness.
4. **Escalating Stakes Ladder**: Progressively escalate consequences: Tier 1 Material Loss ➔ Tier 2 Relational/Social Loss ➔ Tier 3 Existential Identity Crisis.
5. **Sensory Anchoring & Unified Thematic Metaphors**: Anchor concrete physical textures (cold slate, acrid lamp smoke, grit between teeth) unified by a single metaphoric domain (unraveling thread, melting wax, cracking stone).
6. **Circular Epilogue Callback**: The opening sensory image from Beat 1 reappears in Beat 13 with its emotional and spiritual meaning inverted 180°.

### § 3. High-Stakes Verbal Fencing & The 3-Step Reversal Anatomy
- **The Invariant 12-Word Dialogue Ceiling**: In high-tension confrontations, no spoken line may exceed 12 English words (~4.0s). Dialogue acts as kinetic parries, not lectures.
- **The 3-Step Strike Cadence**:
  1. *The Provocation* (Antagonist strikes arrogantly, 4–8 words).
  2. *The 1.5-Second Somatic Gaze Arrest* (Protagonist freezes in cold composure; unblinking eyes, masseter jaw clench; strictly 0 spoken words).
  3. *The Fatal Reversal* (Protagonist delivers the undeniable counter-truth, 6–10 words).
- **The 3 Master Reversal Archetypes** *(genre tropes the audience already expects — list them as expected choices in the Cliché Audit, Doctrine §8.6, and use one only deliberately, with its plants on screen, §9.7)*:
  - *The Concealed Titan (`The Hidden Master`)*: Antagonist mocks humble status ➔ Protagonist activates dormant executive/military authority ➔ Antagonist collapses.
  - *The Sacred Token (`Secret Lineage`)*: Expelled protagonist revealed as rightful imperial heir when royal patriarch arrives.
  - *The Forged Contract Counter-Ambush (`Minute-89 Reversal`)*: Traitor gloats over stolen company/debt ➔ Protagonist reveals the document was an undercover sting operation; enforcers enter.

### § 4. AI Voiceover Directing & Emotional Modulation Tags
When directing synthetic TTS speech (Minimax, ElevenLabs, Piper), script lines must be tagged with directional acoustics:
- `[Coldly]`: Monotone, slow cadence (1.8 wps), lowered pitch, zero breathiness (Protagonist initiating counter-strike).
- `[Thunderous Fury]`: High volume (raised intensity), resonant chest projection, rapid staccato delivery (Antagonist intimidation).
- `[Contemptuous Smirk]`: Raised pitch on syllable endings, casual sneering cadence (Antagonist taunting).
- `[Paralyzed Terror]`: Trembling vibrato, vocal cord constriction, broken breaths (Defeated villain).
- `[Subservient Reverence]`: Low pitch, dignified measured cadence, zero inflection (Loyal guards/assistants).
- `[Righteous Defiance]`: Firm resonant vocal timbre, explosive consonant finishes (Hero resisting tyranny).
- `[Weary Grief]`: Breathy phonation, dropping pitch, trailing cadence on final word (Wounded ally/mentor).
- `[Hushed Secret]`: Near-whisper vocal fry, micro-proximity to microphone, high sibilance (Clandestine informant).
